as recorded in reports/20261014-integration12/wasm.json (the earlier prose
value was a transcription error). Raw/gzip and the budget are unchanged.

The merged 20k Lean campaign found three new completeness disagreements in
simple party pages pinning their leading order column. PAGE4's general positive
classifier bypassed the existing simple classifier's system/party order check.
Apply that same physical-order requirement to the independent compound model:
the authored native scan stays supported and incomplete, including empty and
nullable-order cases; do not hide the cases behind an Unsupported label.
Two model regressions retain readable rows and unknown absence. No runtime,
compiler plan, read authority or device bytes change. Re-run the complete 20k
campaign and formal checks after this model correction.

### 2026-10-14 — FIX28: pre-wave defects in unpublished 0.4.28

Keep 0.4.28. Extend both packed proof and starter-toolchain qualification to
every supported frontend/template pair, twice; require their complete matrices
in imported Linux evidence. Browser execution belongs to the Linux host; clean
Ubuntu/Alpine userspaces still independently build all packed starters. Default
web/Expo adaptation also runs twice. The expanded baseline run exposes web chat
ignoring ?api= as well as the reported Expo chat/social clients; all current
clients now use the Todos runtime URL override. Historical byte templates stay
historical. Generated READMEs explain both proofs, runtime ranges and CHROME.
Native smoke uses the runtime launcher, reading its current range on each run.

Runtime history acknowledgment is independent of successful compiler snapshots.
First check delivers history once even on refusal; upgrade baseline checks cannot
consume it, and install-first cannot overwrite older compiler facts. Refuse an
implicit downgrade before compilation/install, with an explicit --to remedy.
Human upgrade runs human check; --json emits one object and puts progress/history
on stderr. LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and its packaged-proof child record this.

Dormant row populations cover their own retained cells, bytes and assets; only
active rows contribute standalone column cells/samples. The archived-row example
is one row plus two cells. Audit keeps actual restoration counters and distinguishes
a truly empty definition from zero standalone cells covered by a dormant row.
The widening note says rows that later use those values. LLP 3000.000.000 §11
and the deploy card document the disjoint counts. Validation/review follows.

FIX28 review round 1: Muse found code sound but required final evidence; fresh
Codex found historical README recognition lost its old exact bytes and Rust
history lost accepted prerelease suffixes. Recognize old READMEs by ignoring
only the exact generated proof appendix (preserve edits), and distinguish
proof-only advice from offline changes. Align Rust/JS prerelease ordering,
including dotted/numeric suffixes without floating-point precision loss. Real
check regressions verify each stable history entry once after prereleases;
web/Expo regressions verify old README adaptation and edited-file preservation.
The first post-fix qualifier was interrupted through its owned tool session
before completing, so it supplies no successful qualification evidence.

FIX28 social proof closeout: fixing ?api= reveals the social template's Sign out
control is on Profile, while the generic proof remains on Following. A packed
consumer reproduction with only the URL fixed still times out; clicking Profile
through its rendered tab passes twice with occupied-port/CPU load. Emit that
step only for --template social; retain generic defaults/other templates. The
all-template regression checks its selection, and final packed matrices execute
it. The second qualifier was interrupted before completing and claims no pass.

FIX28 schema-less JSON control: its history was present both in a JSON field
and on stderr. Remove the redundant JSON history field; every upgrade/check
JSON path keeps history exclusively on stderr. An actual helper regression
first fails for duplicate output, then verifies one emission and silent repeat.
This is output custody only; no package/version/history interval change.
The prior qualification attempt was interrupted before completing; a fresh
exact packed-client run follows this final helper change.

+  { id: "web", kind: "web", flags: ["--web"] },
+  { id: "web-online", kind: "web", flags: ["--web", "--online"] },
+  { id: "expo", kind: "expo", flags: ["--expo"] },

+];
+async function boundedStop(work, label) {
+  let timer;
+  try { return await Promise.race([work, new Promise((_, reject) => { timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(label + " timed out")), 10_000); })]); }
+  finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
+}
+export async function proveFrontendTwice(run, cwd, starter) {
+  const results = [];
+  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= 2; attempt++) {
+    const occupied = createServer();
+    const low = await new Promise((resolve, reject) => { occupied.once("error", reject); occupied.listen(0, "127.0.0.1", () => resolve(occupied.address().port)); });
+    const high = Math.min(65535, low + 20);
+    const workers = Array.from({ length: 2 }, () => new Worker(`setInterval(() => { const until = performance.now() + 25; while (performance.now() < until) {} }, 50);`, { eval: true }));
+    const started = Date.now(), load = loadavg();
+    try {
+      await run("npm", ["run", "frontend:proof"], cwd, { PORT_LO: String(low), PORT_HI: String(high) });
+      results.push({ starter: starter.id, kind: starter.kind, attempt, status: "passed", elapsedMs: Date.now() - started, range: [low, high], occupied: low, cpuLoadWorkers: workers.length, loadavg: load, logs: join(cwd, "logs") });
+    } finally {
+      const stopped = await Promise.allSettled([...workers.map(worker => boundedStop(worker.terminate(), "CPU worker stop")), boundedStop(new Promise(resolve => occupied.close(resolve)), "occupied listener close")]);
+      const failures = stopped.filter(result => result.status === "rejected").map(result => result.reason);
+      if (failures.length) throw new AggregateError(failures, "loaded-host proof cleanup failed");
+    }
+  }
+  return results;
+}

import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, renameSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
const version = value => /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(value ?? '') ? value : undefined;
export function lockedVersion(text) {
  try {
    const lock = JSON.parse(text);
    const found = version(lock.packages?.['node_modules/snapback4']?.version ?? lock.dependencies?.snapback4?.version);
    if (found) return found;
  } catch { /* Bun lockfiles are JSONC; pnpm is YAML. */ }
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  const found = new Set();
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) if (/^\s*['"]?snapback4['"]?:\s*$/.test(lines[i])) {
    for (const line of lines.slice(i + 1, i + 5)) {
      const resolved = /^\s*version:\s*(.+)$/.exec(line)?.[1]?.trim().replace(/^['"]|['"]$/g, '').split('(')[0];
      if (version(resolved)) found.add(resolved);
    }
  }
  for (const match of text.matchAll(/snapback4@([^\s"'/:()[\]{},]+)/g)) if (version(match[1])) found.add(match[1]);
  return found.size === 1 ? [...found][0] : undefined;
}
export function installedVersion(root) {
  try { const require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json')); return version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version); } catch { /* Older copied trees may have no installed compiler. */ }
  return undefined;
}
export function previousVersion(root) {
  for (const name of ['runtime-version.json', 'check-surface.json', 'upgrade-version.json']) {
    try { const found = version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, '.snapback4', name), 'utf8')).version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent or invalid */ }
  }
  try { const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'snapback/generated/package.json'), 'utf8')); const found = version(pkg.snapback4Version ?? pkg.version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent */ }
  for (const name of ['package-lock.json', 'npm-shrinkwrap.json', 'bun.lock', 'pnpm-lock.yaml']) {
    try { const found = lockedVersion(readFileSync(join(root, name), 'utf8')); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent */ }
  }
  return installedVersion(root);
}
export function acknowledgeVersion(root, version) {
  const directory = join(root, '.snapback4'); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true });
  const path = join(directory, 'runtime-version.json'), temporary = `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`;
  writeFileSync(temporary, JSON.stringify({ version })); renameSync(temporary, path);
}
export function compareVersions(a, b) {
  const parts = v => { const i = v.indexOf('-'); return i < 0 ? [v, undefined] : [v.slice(0, i), v.slice(i + 1)]; };
  const [ac, ap] = parts(a), [bc, bp] = parts(b);
  const av = ac.split('.').map(Number), bv = bc.split('.').map(Number);
  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) if (av[i] !== bv[i]) return av[i] - bv[i];
  if (ap === undefined || bp === undefined) return ap === bp ? 0 : ap === undefined ? 1 : -1;
  const aa = ap.split('.'), ba = bp.split('.');
  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(aa.length, ba.length); i++) {
    if (aa[i] === ba[i]) continue;
    if (aa[i] === undefined || ba[i] === undefined) return aa[i] === undefined ? -1 : 1;
    const an = /^\d+$/.test(aa[i]), bn = /^\d+$/.test(ba[i]);
    if (an && bn) { const av = aa[i].replace(/^0+/, ''), bv = ba[i].replace(/^0+/, ''); if (av === bv) continue; return av.length - bv.length || (av < bv ? -1 : 1); }
    if (an !== bn) return an ? -1 : 1;
    return aa[i] < ba[i] ? -1 : 1;
  }
  return 0;
}
export function runtimeChanges(history, previous, installed) {
  if (!version(previous) || !version(installed) || compareVersions(previous, installed) >= 0) return '';
  let selected = false, out = '';
  for (const line of history.split('\n')) {
    if (line.startsWith('## ')) { const v = line.slice(3); selected = !!version(v) && compareVersions(v, previous) > 0 && compareVersions(v, installed) <= 0; }
    if (selected && line.startsWith('## ')) out += (out ? '\n' : '') + line + '\n\n';
    else if (selected && line.startsWith('- ')) out += line + '\n';
  }
  return out ? `runtime changes: ${previous} → ${installed}\n${out}History begins at 0.4.26; earlier changes are not recorded.\n` : '';
}
export function installedHistory(root) {
  // The parent process resolved the old package already. Resolve the new
  // symlink in a fresh process, just like upgrade's post-install check.
  const script = `import {createRequire} from 'node:module';import {join,dirname} from 'node:path';const require=createRequire(join(process.argv[1],'package.json'));console.log(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))),'CHANGELOG.md'));`;
  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, ['--input-type=module', '-e', script, root], { encoding: 'utf8' });
  if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw Error('installed release history unavailable');
  return readFileSync(result.stdout.trim(), 'utf8');
}

//! A compiler upgrade explains the delta against the previous successful check.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#9 — snapshots explain versions; failed compilation cannot replace the old API.
use super::*;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::{fs, path::PathBuf};

#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize, Default)]
struct Surface {
    version: String,
    #[serde(default)] sources: Option<Vec<(String, String)>>,
    api: String,
    notes: BTreeMap<String, usize>,
    refusals: BTreeMap<String, usize>,
}
fn path(project: &Project) -> PathBuf { project.data_dir().join("check-surface.json") }
fn counts(notes: &[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic]) -> BTreeMap<String, usize> {
    let mut counts = BTreeMap::new();
    for note in notes { *counts.entry(note.code.clone()).or_default() += 1; }
    counts
}
fn current(project: &Project, bound: &Bound) -> Surface {
    Surface { version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").into(), sources: project.sources().ok(),
        api: if bound.diagnostics.is_empty() { snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(bound) } else { String::new() },
        notes: counts(&bound.notes), refusals: counts(&bound.diagnostics) }
}
/// The emitter's own fixed layout: row columns and complete operation signatures.
/// Inline object types stay on one line; comments and runtime api objects are excluded.
fn types(api: &str) -> BTreeMap<String, String> {
    let mut scope = Vec::new();
    let mut types = BTreeMap::new();
    for line in api.lines().map(str::trim) {
        if line.starts_with("export const ") || line.starts_with("const operations:") { break; }
        if let Some(name) = line.strip_prefix("export type ").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(" = {")) {
            scope.clear(); scope.push(name.to_owned()); continue;
        }
        if line == "};" { scope.pop(); continue; }
        if !matches!(scope.first().map(String::as_str), Some("Rows" | "Ops")) { continue; }
        if let Some(name) = line.strip_suffix(": {") { scope.push(name.trim_matches('"').into()); continue; }
        if line.starts_with('/') { continue; }
        if let Some((name, value)) = line.strip_suffix(';').and_then(|s| s.split_once(": ")) {
            let key = scope.iter().map(String::as_str).chain([name.trim_matches('"')]).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(".");
            if scope[0] == "Ops" {
                if let Some((args, result)) = operation_types(value) {
                    types.insert(format!("{key}.args"), args); types.insert(format!("{key}.result"), result); continue;
                }
            }
            types.insert(key, value.into());
        }
    }
    types
}
fn operation_types(value: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {
    let body = value.split_once('<')?.1.strip_suffix('>')?;
    let mut depth = 0i32;
    for (index, ch) in body.char_indices() {
        match ch {
            '{' | '[' | '<' | '(' => depth += 1,
            '}' | ']' | '>' | ')' => depth -= 1,
            ',' if depth == 0 => return Some((body[..index].trim().into(), body[index + 1..].trim().into())),
            _ => {},
        }
    }
    None
}

pub(super) fn report(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, json: bool) {
    let old: Surface = match fs::read(path(project)).ok().and_then(|b| serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()) { Some(old) => old, None => {
        return;
    } };
    let new = current(project, bound);
    if old.version == new.version { return; }
    let mut lines = vec![format!("upgrade delta: {} → {} for snapback/*.q{}", old.version, new.version,
        if old.sources.is_some() && old.sources != new.sources { " (schema also changed)" } else { "" })];
    for (label, before, after) in [("new notes", &old.notes, &new.notes), ("new refusals", &old.refusals, &new.refusals)] {
        let added = after.iter().filter_map(|(code, count)| {
            let delta = count.saturating_sub(*before.get(code).unwrap_or(&0));
            (delta > 0).then(|| format!("{code} +{delta}"))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        lines.push(format!("  {label}: {}", if added.is_empty() { "none".into() } else { added.join(", ") }));
    }
    if new.api.is_empty() { lines.push("  generated/api.ts: unavailable until the schema compiles; previous types kept".into()); }
    else {
        let before = types(&old.api); let after = types(&new.api);
        let keys: std::collections::BTreeSet<_> = before.keys().chain(after.keys()).collect();
        let mut changed = false;
        for key in keys {
            if before.get(key) == after.get(key) { continue; }
            changed = true;
            lines.push(format!("  generated/api.ts {key}: {} → {}", before.get(key).map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("(added)"), after.get(key).map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("(removed)")));
        }
        if !changed { lines.push("  generated/api.ts types: unchanged".into()); }
    }
    lines.push("  release card: snapback4 guide changes".into());
    for line in lines { if json { eprintln!("{line}"); } else { println!("{line}"); } }
}

pub(super) fn remember(project: &Project, bound: &Bound) {
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() || std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_BASELINE").as_deref() == Ok("1") { return; }
    let save = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
        fs::create_dir_all(project.data_dir())?;
        let temporary = path(project).with_extension(format!("{}.tmp", std::process::id()));
        fs::write(&temporary, serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&current(project, bound))?)?;
        fs::rename(temporary, path(project))?;
        let _ = fs::remove_file(project.data_dir().join("upgrade-version.json"));
        Ok(())
    };
    if let Err(error) = save() { eprintln!("check: upgrade comparison not saved: {error}"); }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn generated_surface_separates_modules_columns_and_nested_types() {
        let api = "export type Rows = {\n  things: {\n    id: string;\n    total: number | null;\n  };\n};\nexport type Ops = {\n  stats: {\n    count: Query<{ room: string; labels: Array<string> }, number | null>;\n  };\n};\nexport const api: Ops = {\n  fake: 42;\n};";
        let types = types(api);
        assert_eq!(types["Rows.things.total"], "number | null");
        assert_eq!(types["Ops.stats.count.result"], "number | null");
        assert_eq!(types.len(), 4);
    }
}
//! Recover a version, not invented compiler facts, from an older app.
use super::*;
use std::fs;
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#11-wave-11-runtime-upgrade-history — history acknowledgment is independent of compiler qualification.

fn exact(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    snapback4_lang::release_changes::version(text).map(|_| text.to_owned())
}
fn json_version(text: &str, key: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let value: Json = serde_json::from_str(text).ok()?;
    exact(value[key].as_str()?)
}
pub(super) fn locked_version(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    if let Ok(lock) = serde_json::from_str::<Json>(text) {
        if let Some(version) = lock.pointer("/packages/node_modules~1snapback4/version").or_else(|| lock.pointer("/dependencies/snapback4/version")).and_then(Json::as_str).and_then(exact) { return Some(version); }
    }
    // Bun's JSONC package tuple; pnpm's importer resolution (not specifier).
    let mut versions = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    for (position, _) in text.match_indices("snapback4@") {
        let tail = &text[position + "snapback4@".len()..];
        let candidate = tail.split(|c: char| !(c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(c, '.' | '-'))).next().unwrap_or("");
        if let Some(version) = exact(candidate) { versions.insert(version); }
    }
    let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
        if line.trim().trim_matches('\'').trim_matches('"') == "snapback4:" {
            for line in lines.iter().skip(index + 1).take(4) {
                if let Some(value) = line.trim().strip_prefix("version:") {
                    let value = value.trim().trim_matches(['\'', '"']).split('(').next().unwrap_or("");
                    if let Some(version) = exact(value) { versions.insert(version); }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    (versions.len() == 1).then(|| versions.into_iter().next()).flatten()
}
pub(super) fn previous(project: &Project) -> Option<String> {
    let read_version = |path, key| fs::read_to_string(path).ok().and_then(|text| json_version(&text, key));
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("runtime-version.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("check-surface.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("upgrade-version.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.generated_dir().join("package.json"), "snapback4Version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.generated_dir().join("package.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    for name in ["package-lock.json", "npm-shrinkwrap.json", "bun.lock", "pnpm-lock.yaml"] {
        if let Some(version) = fs::read_to_string(project.root.join(name)).ok().and_then(|text| locked_version(&text)) { return Some(version); }
    }
    None
}
pub(super) fn report(project: &Project, json: bool) {
    if std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_BASELINE").as_deref() == Ok("1") { return; }
    if let Some(previous) = previous(project) {
        let text = snapback4_lang::release_changes::interval(&previous, env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
        if !text.is_empty() { if json { eprint!("{text}"); } else { print!("{text}"); } }
    }
    let save = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
        fs::create_dir_all(project.data_dir())?;
        let path = project.data_dir().join("runtime-version.json");
        let temporary = path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp", std::process::id()));
        fs::write(&temporary, serde_json::to_vec(&json!({ "version": env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION") }))?)?;
        fs::rename(temporary, path)
    };
    if let Err(error) = save() { eprintln!("check: runtime history acknowledgment not saved: {error}"); }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn lock_resolution_uses_versions_not_dependency_ranges() {
        for text in [r#"{"packages":{"node_modules/snapback4":{"version":"0.4.25"}}}"#, r#"{"dependencies":{"snapback4":{"version":"0.4.25"}}}"#, "{\n\"snapback4\": [\"snapback4@0.4.25\", \"\", {}, \"sha\"],\n}", "importers:\n  .:\n    dependencies:\n      snapback4:\n        specifier: ^0.4.24\n        version: 0.4.25", "packages:\n  snapback4@0.4.25:\n    resolution: {}"] {
            assert_eq!(locked_version(text).as_deref(), Some("0.4.25"), "{text}");
        }
        for text in ["snapback4@latest", "snapback4@0.4.24\nsnapback4@0.4.25", "snapback4:\n  specifier: ^0.4.25", "snapback4@0.4.25.1", "importers:\n  one:\n    snapback4:\n      version: 0.4.25\n  two:\n    snapback4:\n      version: 0.4.27"] { assert!(locked_version(text).is_none(), "{text}"); }
    }
}

## 10. Expo web-export qualification — 2026-10-14 (EXPOFIX)

Published 0.4.26's Expo starters configured an SVG favicon. Expo's image
pipeline rejects that input during `expo export -p web`, although the
starter-content tests passed. Remove the optional favicon key and unused SVG
from every Expo starter (Todos, chat and social); keep the SVG in Vite's
public directory, where the browser consumes it directly. An application can
supply its own raster favicon. `init --adapt` removes only the exact generated
SVG and its exact setting, preserving custom images, paths and other config.
Fresh UI starters describe the known Todos schema's offline behavior immediately;
`check` must issue no adaptation advice on any untouched starter.

The publication's installed-consumer proof now installs packed CLI/client and
Expo packages, generates the real web and Expo starters, installs their pinned
toolchain dependencies, runs `check` and typecheck, builds Vite, and runs both
`expo export -p web` and the starter's `build:web`. It also adapts each to an
online-only Home schema and repeats the builds, then installs/typechecks/builds
the web-chat, Expo-chat and social templates. Proofs have a twenty-minute
per-distribution guard; individual commands have five minutes. Expo workers
are bounded at two in the direct export and generated build:web scripts;
unbounded worker pools made the namespace-free proof exceed its command guard. Dependency installation can access
the npm registry with prefer-offline caching; Expo's network checks and
telemetry are disabled. This web proof does not qualify native iOS binaries.

`--prepare-client` also packs Expo as a proof input. Linux qualification requires
`--expo <prepared-expo.tgz>` and binds its SRI plus web/Expo/adapt success in
both Ubuntu and Alpine proof records. The publisher checks that SRI against
its preparation and retains those exact Expo bytes in its stage. Darwin-only
publication and resume run the same starter proof. Expo's native release
publication remains the existing separate native qualification path; this
change does not publish an unqualified native package.

FIX28 (2026-10-14) closes the browser gap: the starter-toolchain step also runs
the generated frontend:proof twice for every supported frontend/template pair,
including web --online, and twice after adapting default web/Expo to Home.
README recognition ignores only the exact generated proof appendix, so pre-FIX28
untouched READMEs remain owned starter bytes and receive current proof/screen/identity
instructions on adaptation. Edits to the appendix or surrounding README remain owned
by the application.
These browser attempts run on the qualification host, with occupied first ports
and CPU load, and their complete evidence is required alongside the PROOF
matrix in new Linux receipts. Ubuntu/Alpine remain isolated build/toolchain
consumers (`--build-only`); no host browser or shared libraries enter their
userspaces. Darwin runs the same complete starter/browser step.

The audit covers all emitted assets/config: Expo's app ids, plugins, entrypoint,
Babel/Metro configs, wasm transformer and dependency versions, Vite's module
config, HTML, public SVG and generated offline shell. No other emitted Expo
image key exists. The changed-schema builds exercise journal/device selection,
generated API and adapted Home/sign-in/README paths. Chat/social receive their own
real installs and exports: social has a Router entrypoint and extra plugins.
The audit found its floating Router range selecting an unqualified release
with an unavailable dependency during install; pin Router to 57.0.23, the
version in the existing workspace lock, and qualify it through the same path.


Historical publication stages without a retained Expo proof input are refused
with a specific explanation, preserving all artifacts. A new version closes
the gap; a pre-gap suite receipt cannot authorize publication without the new
real-toolchain evidence. Format-2 stage layout remains readable when it carries
the required Expo bytes; tests distinguish that from genuine old stages.


The command guard uses the existing live command-owner keeper. Only that
keeper signals its own process group; completion and timeout close its private
pipe, and caller death does the same. Commands are asynchronous so npm's
signal handling cannot block the watchdog. Success requires the command's
zero exit plus two empty group-existence observations; lingering members or
inspection errors refuse. Clean userspaces need no host /proc mount. Cleanup
allows up to five seconds beyond the command guard; deliberately detached
processes are outside group custody, as in the existing suite contract.


EXPOFIX final review record: both
[Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-expofix.round4.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-expofix.round4.sol.md) are READY on
74d144a326, including the process-group custody change. Earlier actual reviews,
the capacity-failed session and all briefs remain verbatim under reviews/.
The first Codex round's unbounded-worker/timeout counterexample produced the
worker cap and live-keeper repair; historical-stage behavior is now explicit.
No full publication receipt, native qualification or upload is claimed here.

## 11. Wave 11 runtime upgrade history

Commission and implementer: Charlie Cheever's NARR workstream, Codex,
2026-10-14; target 0.4.28. This amends §9's missing-baseline and changes-card
behavior; it changes neither mutation settlement nor custody. Review together
with LLP 3000.000.000 §11 and LLP 3000.001.000's NARR diagnostics.

A packaged CHANGELOG.md records behavior by release, including client runtime
semantics. The compiler build generates `guide changes` through its own version
from this text. Entries backfill 0.4.26's `useMutation().run()` terminal
settlement (offline waits for reconnect; waitSeconds: 0 returns custody) and
0.4.27's Expo/device fixes and unchanged settlement semantics. History starts
at 0.4.26; an earlier baseline receives all recorded entries with that limit
stated. A future release must add its meaningful runtime changes to this same
file; NARR's 0.4.28 entry covers this lane only, and integration owns the
other lanes' final release entries.

First check prints entries in (previous, installed] before device preflight,
including when schema/device qualification fails. JSON remains one stdout
object; in JSON mode history goes to stderr, and ordinary human output uses stdout. Compiler comparison still requires
its real successful snapshot. A missing compiler snapshot creates no invented
old types/notes/refusals. Version recovery tries the check snapshot, a failed
upgrade's recorded previous version, generated package version metadata, then
npm/Bun/pnpm lockfile resolutions. Ranges are not resolved versions; ambiguous
lockfile versions are unavailable. New generated/package.json includes a
snapback4Version alongside its ESM marker so copied apps retain a baseline.
Failed checks preserve the prior snapshot; successful check remembers the new
compiler surface and removes the temporary upgrade-version record. FIX28 keeps
`.snapback4/runtime-version.json` separately: the last version whose history was
delivered, including on a refused check. History is shown exactly once; a failed
compiler check still retains the successful surface snapshot. The acknowledgment
is atomically written after delivering history (or initializing a missing baseline).
This acknowledgment takes precedence over surface/temporary/generated/lock versions.
Internal upgrade baseline checks suppress history and surface acknowledgment.
Rust and JavaScript use prerelease-aware ordering: a stable release follows all
its prereleases, dotted numeric identifiers compare numerically without integer
precision loss, and first check after a prerelease includes the stable entry.
An install performed first cannot replace the real older compiler snapshot;
upgrade captures compiler facts only when the installed compiler matches the
acknowledged version. Human upgrade uses human check output; --json returns one
object with package-manager/progress/history output on stderr. Latest or a legacy
positional target cannot downgrade an installed version: only --to <exact version>
authorizes that decision, checked before baseline compilation or install.

Upgrade captures the previous installed package (or lock/generated metadata)
before installation. If old check refuses, it records only that version; new
check can still narrate runtime changes. Without Quarry source upgrade reads
the newly installed package's changelog after symlink replacement and reports
the same interval. It preserves existing package pin/section rules and names
Exact's rebuild command. Rebuild is advice, not an automatic Exact mutation.

A development-only run observer warns once per call after ten continuous
seconds offline while its result is still pending. Reconnect cancels the timer;
another offline interval starts it again until the first warning. Terminal
settlement or rejection clears listeners/timer even after component unmount.
waitSeconds: 0 owns no settlement timer. Development is explicit __DEV__ or NODE_ENV development/test, using the
standard expression bundlers replace. Unknown unbundled browser environments
remain silent; no import.meta syntax or Hermes polyfill is introduced. The warning explains terminal settlement,
reconnect and the custody alternative; it does not cancel or retry a write.

P9 advice is small: a device-planned cursorless page over public unbounded sync,
scoped by a reference outside the sync audience, gets N_SYNC_EMPTY naming the lack of a bounded empty-partition acquisition
proof and a bounded/unfiltered-window or explicit server-client alternative. It changes
no planner/runtime/protocol and does not assert that every empty result stalls;
the analysis verified missing check advice but did not reproduce the runtime
claim. PAGE4 owns planner reach and completeness proofs. Positive and negative
compiler controls bind that distinction; runtime reach is not NARR's claim.

Acceptance: older npm/Bun/pnpm and generated-only baselines, refused old check,
same-version repeat, preflight refusal, exact interval filtering and shared
Rust/JS history text; fake-timer tests for offline threshold/reconnect/production,
terminal cleanup and custody; query-note scoped/bounded/server controls.

`f70dc90f4d` READY in round 2. Verbatim artifacts, repaired findings and the
scoped verification record are linked from LLP 3000.000.000's NARR readiness
section. Additional review controls change tests only. Round 3 from both families
marks the generic widening wording clarification at 3b770ea426 READY; it changes
no decision logic. No publication or complete-battery claim follows from readiness.

 Preview names missing operations, changed rule clauses, absent schedules and populated removed tables: dormant holds no longer enforce their safeguards. A changed job body is named separately; an unchanged job that compiles differently after a dependency change gets a note. Captures from the original read-through lifetime stay dormant with columns; restoring the same capture definition restores them. New rows without those captures still need safe audience correspondence.
 
-Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 3 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [A, B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.
+Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.
 
 ## Clients across rollback
 Generated required fields and operations describe the schema checked when the client was built. Rolling the server back does not rebuild that client. Choose a release-matched frontend: in a separate copy of the selected immutable source, run `snapback4 check`, rebuild the frontend against that generated API and deploy it alongside the backend. Keep the immutable deployment source intact. An Expo/Exact runtime change also needs a device rebuild; JavaScript reload alone does not rebuild it.

+
+// The installed package facade changes version as npm would; the current check
+// runs the actual binary, so acknowledgment and surface writes are real.
+function upgradeFixture(t, installed = releaseVersion, latest = releaseVersion) {
+  const dir = project(t, 'fix28-upgrade-'); mkdirSync(join(dir, 'snapback'));
+  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'snapback/schema.q'), aggregates); success(invoke(['check'], dir));
+  const surface = join(dir, '.snapback4/check-surface.json');
+  writeFileSync(surface, JSON.stringify({ ...JSON.parse(readFileSync(surface)), version: '0.4.27' }));
+  writeFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/runtime-version.json'), '{"version":"0.4.27"}');
+  const pack = join(dir, 'node_modules/snapback4'); mkdirSync(join(pack, 'dist'), { recursive: true }); mkdirSync(join(pack, 'bin'));
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'dist/bin-path.js'), '');
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'snapback4', version: installed, exports: { './bin-path': './dist/bin-path.js' } }));
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'CHANGELOG.md'), readFileSync(new URL('../CHANGELOG.md', import.meta.url)));
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'bin/snapback4.js'), `const r=require('child_process').spawnSync(${JSON.stringify(bin)},process.argv.slice(2),{encoding:'utf8'});process.stdout.write(r.stdout);process.stderr.write(r.stderr);process.exit(r.status);`);
+  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ dependencies: { snapback4: installed }, packageManager: 'npm@10' }));
+  mkdirSync(join(dir, 'tools')); writeFileSync(join(dir, 'tools/npm'), `#!${process.execPath}
+const fs=require('fs');if(process.argv[2]==='view')console.log('${JSON.stringify(latest)}');else{fs.appendFileSync('installs','install\\n');const v=process.argv.at(-1).split('@').at(-1);for(const file of ['package.json','node_modules/snapback4/package.json']){const p=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file));if(p.dependencies)p.dependencies.snapback4=v;else p.version=v;fs.writeFileSync(file,JSON.stringify(p));}}`, { mode: 0o755 });
+  return { dir, env: { PATH: `${join(dir, 'tools')}:${process.env.PATH}` } };
+}
+for (const order of ['upgrade-first', 'install-then-upgrade', 'install-then-check']) for (const json of [false, true]) test(`runtime history is acknowledged once: ${order}, ${json ? 'JSON' : 'human'}`, t => {
+  const { dir, env } = upgradeFixture(t, order === 'upgrade-first' ? '0.4.27' : releaseVersion);
+  const first = invoke([order === 'install-then-check' ? 'check' : 'upgrade', ...(json ? ['--json'] : [])], dir, env); success(first);
+  const stream = first.stdout + first.stderr;
+  assert.equal((stream.match(/runtime changes: 0\.4\.27 →/g) ?? []).length, 1, stream);
+  if (json) assert.equal(JSON.parse(first.stdout).ok, true);
+  else { assert.doesNotMatch(first.stdout, /"problems"\s*:/); assert.match(first.stdout, /check:/); }
+  const again = invoke(['check'], dir, env); success(again);
+  const upgrade = invoke(['upgrade'], dir, env); success(upgrade);
+  assert.doesNotMatch(again.stdout + again.stderr + upgrade.stdout + upgrade.stderr, /runtime changes:/);
+  assert.equal(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/runtime-version.json'))).version, releaseVersion);
+});
+test('registry latest cannot silently downgrade; only --to authorizes it', t => {
+  const { dir, env } = upgradeFixture(t, releaseVersion, '0.4.26');
+  const before = readFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/check-surface.json'), 'utf8');
+  for (const args of [['upgrade'], ['upgrade', '0.4.26']]) {
+    const refused = invoke(args, dir, env); assert.equal(refused.status, 1); assert.match(refused.stderr, /older than installed.*refusing a downgrade.*--to 0.4.26/);
+    assert.equal(readFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/check-surface.json'), 'utf8'), before);
+    assert.equal(readFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'), 'utf8').includes(releaseVersion), true);
+  }
+  success(invoke(['upgrade', '--to', '0.4.26'], dir, env));
+  assert.equal(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'))).dependencies.snapback4, '0.4.26');
+});
+
+test('schema-less JSON upgrade emits runtime history on stderr exactly once', t => {
+  const { dir, env } = upgradeFixture(t);
+  rmSync(join(dir, 'snapback/schema.q'));
+  const result = invoke(['upgrade', '--json'], dir, env); success(result);
+  assert.equal(JSON.parse(result.stdout).ok, true);
+  assert.doesNotMatch(result.stdout, /runtime changes:/);
+  assert.equal((result.stderr.match(/runtime changes: 0\.4\.27 →/g) ?? []).length, 1, result.stderr);
+  const again = invoke(['upgrade', '--json'], dir, env); success(again);
+  assert.doesNotMatch(again.stdout + again.stderr, /runtime changes:/);
+});

//! Database-authoritative release history and explicit dormant pruning.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#4-explicit-pruning-and-audit — no implicit collector or unaudited data loss.
use super::*;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DormantItem {
    /// Stable cursor; column/index names may coincide.
    pub key: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub kind: String,
    pub action: String,
    pub rows: u64,
    pub bytes: u64,
    pub asset_bytes: u64,
    /// Internal audit distinction: zero standalone cells may still be covered by a dormant row.
    #[serde(skip)]
    pub(crate) definition_only: bool,
    pub sample_ids: Vec<String>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub before_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub after_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub restored_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub before_sample_ids:Option<Vec<String>>,
}

impl DormantItem {
    pub fn unit(&self) -> &'static str {
        match (self.kind.as_str(), self.rows) { ("column", 1) => "cell", ("column", _) => "cells", ("index", 1) => "entry", ("index", _) => "entries", (_, 1) => "row", _ => "rows" }
    }
}

impl Store {
    /// Values presently in active rows, independently of table population or other columns.
    pub fn rollback_incompatible_values(&self, current: &Schema, target: &Schema, name: &str, column: &str) -> Result<(u64, Vec<String>), RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.authority_transaction()?;
        let table = &target.tables[name];
        let kind = &table.columns[column];
        if table.engine_field(column) || table.maintained.contains_key(column) { return Ok((0, Vec::new())); }
        let mut count = 0;
        let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        let mut statement = tx.conn().prepare("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1) ORDER BY id")?;
        for data in statement.query_map([name], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))? {
            let mut row: Value = serde_json::from_str(&data?).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, &current.tables[name]);
            if let Ok(value) = row.field(column)
                && super::narrowed::incompatible_column(&tx, table, name, column, kind, value)? {
                count += 1;
                if samples.len() < 5 {
                    let text = match value { Value::String(s) => format!("'{}'", s.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'").replace('\n', "\\n").replace('\r', "\\r")), _ => canonical_json(value) };
                    samples.insert(super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&text));
                }
            }
        }
        Ok((count, samples.into_iter().collect()))
    }
}

fn sql_number(n: u64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    i64::try_from(n).map_err(|_| {
        RuntimeError::Generation("release counter exceeds SQLite integer range".into())
    })
}

pub(super) fn present(conn: &Connection) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(conn.query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type='table' AND name='_dormant_tables')",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?)
}
pub(crate) fn inventory(
    conn: &Connection,
    after: Option<&str>,
    limit: usize,
) -> Result<Vec<DormantItem>, RuntimeError> {
    if !present(conn)? {
        return Ok(Vec::new());
    }
    let narrowed = if super::narrowed::present(conn)? {
        " UNION ALL SELECT c.name,'rows',c.name,NULL FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t GROUP BY n.t "
    } else {""};
    let query=format!("SELECT path,kind,table_name,column_name FROM (
        SELECT table_name AS path,'table' AS kind,table_name,NULL AS column_name FROM _dormant_tables
        UNION ALL SELECT table_name||'.'||column_name,'column',table_name,column_name FROM _dormant_columns c WHERE table_name NOT IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog t ON t.num=v.t WHERE t.name=c.table_name AND v.column_name=c.column_name)
        UNION ALL SELECT table_name||'.'||index_name,'index',table_name,index_name FROM _dormant_indexes {narrowed})
        WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR kind||':'||path>?1) ORDER BY kind||':'||path LIMIT ?2");
    let mut statement=conn.prepare(&query)?;
    let items: Vec<(String, String, String, Option<String>)> = statement
        .query_map(
            params![after, i64::try_from(limit).unwrap_or(i64::MAX)],
            |r| Ok((r.get(0)?, r.get(1)?, r.get(2)?, r.get(3)?)),
        )?
        .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for (path, kind, name, column) in items {
        let t: Option<i64> = conn
            .query_row("SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1", [&name], |r| {
                r.get(0)
            })
            .optional()?;
        let Some(t) = t else { continue };
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — dormant row images cover their retained cells; standalone cells have active owners.
        let (mut count,mut bytes):(u64,u64)=match kind.as_str(){
            "table"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "rows"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "column"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_values v WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id)",params![t,column],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            _=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),0 FROM (SELECT id FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1)",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
        };
        if matches!(kind.as_str(), "table" | "rows") {
            let images = if kind == "table" { "_dormant_rows" } else { "_dormant_narrow_rows" };
            bytes += conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(v.id)+octet_length(v.data)),0) FROM _dormant_values v WHERE v.t=?1 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM {images} d WHERE d.t=v.t AND d.id=v.id)"), [t], |r| r.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64;
        }
        let query = match kind.as_str() {
            "rows" => "SELECT id FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "table" => "SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "column" => {
                "SELECT id FROM _dormant_values v WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id) ORDER BY id LIMIT 5"
            }
            _ => {
                "SELECT id FROM (SELECT id FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1) WHERE ?2 IS NOT NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5"
            }
        };
        let mut ids = conn
            .prepare(query)?
            .query_map(params![t, column], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))?
            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?
            .into_iter()
            .map(|id| super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&id))
            .collect();
        if kind == "index" {
            (count, ids) = index_entries(conn, t, &name, column.as_deref().unwrap())?;
        }
        let asset_bytes = if kind == "index" {
            0
        } else {
            let selector=match kind.as_str(){
                "rows"=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=?3 AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",
                "column"=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN r ON r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id WHERE v.t=?3 AND v.id=_assets.home_id AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column)",
                _=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_rows d WHERE d.t=?3 AND d.id=_assets.home_id)",
            };
            conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(bytes),0) FROM _assets WHERE home_table=?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR home_column=?2) AND released_at IS NULL AND {selector}"),params![name,if kind=="table" || kind=="rows" {None}else{column.as_deref()},t],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?
        };
        let definition_only = if kind == "column" {
            !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2)", params![t, column], |r| r.get::<_, bool>(0))?
        } else { count == 0 };
        out.push(DormantItem {
            key: format!("{kind}:{path}"),
            path,
            kind,
            action: "dormant".into(),
            rows: count,
            bytes,
            asset_bytes,
            definition_only,
            sample_ids: ids,
            before_rows:None,after_rows:None,restored_rows:None,before_sample_ids:None,
        });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// Retained definitions represent logical entries, rebuilt on restoration.
/// A words index has zero or many entries per image; a row count is not its size.
fn index_entries(conn: &Connection, t: i64, table: &str, index: &str) -> Result<(u64, Vec<String>), RuntimeError> {
    let definition: String = conn.query_row("SELECT definition FROM _dormant_indexes WHERE table_name=?1 AND index_name=?2", params![table, index], |r| r.get(0))?;
    let index: crate::schema::Index = serde_json::from_str(&definition).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
    let schema: Option<String> = conn.query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')", [], |r| r.get(0)).optional()?;
    let schema: Option<Schema> = schema.map(|s| serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))).transpose()?;
    let query = if super::narrowed::present(conn)? {
        "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id"
    } else { "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id" };
    let mut count = 0;
    let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let mut statement = conn.prepare(query)?;
    for image in statement.query_map([t], |r| Ok((r.get::<_, String>(0)?, r.get::<_, String>(1)?)))? {
        let (id, data) = image?;
        let mut row: Value = serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
        if let Some(table) = schema.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.table(table)) { crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table); }
        for cell in conn.prepare("SELECT column_name,data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2")?.query_map(params![t, id], |r| Ok((r.get::<_, String>(0)?, r.get::<_, String>(1)?)))? {
            let (column, value) = cell?;
            if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
                fields.entry(column).or_insert(serde_json::from_str(&value).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?);
            }
        }
        let entries = crate::rows::index_keys(&index, &row, &id).len() as u64;
        count += entries;
        if entries > 0 && samples.len() < 5 { samples.insert(super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&id)); }
    }
    Ok((count, samples.into_iter().collect()))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#91-per-row-narrowed-value-dormancy — only rows surviving every activation phase count as restored.
pub(crate) fn finalize_restorations(tx:&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let entries=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%'")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,String>(0)?,r.get::<_,String>(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>()?;
    let mut narrowed=0u64;
    for (key,ids) in entries {
        let (prefix,path)=key.split_once(':').expect("activation restoration key");
        let table=if prefix=="activation_column_restored_ids" {path.split_once('.').expect("column path").0} else {path};
        let count:u64=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?2))",params![tx.num(table)?,ids],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?;
        tx.set_meta(&format!("{}:{path}",prefix.trim_end_matches("_ids")),&count.to_string())?;
        if prefix=="activation_narrowed_restored_ids" {narrowed+=count;}
    }
    tx.set_meta("activation_narrowed_restored",&narrowed.to_string())?;
    Ok(())
}

pub(crate) fn event(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    operation: &str,
    release: Option<u64>,
    previous: Option<u64>,
    summary: serde_json::Value,
    items: &[DormantItem],
) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let revision = tx
        .meta("release_revision")?
        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(0)
        .checked_add(1)
        .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("release revision exhausted".into()))?;
    tx.set_meta("release_revision", &revision.to_string())?;
    let generation = tx
        .meta("generation")?
        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(1);
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _release_events(at,operation,release,previous,generation,revision,summary) VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7)",params![crate::now_ms(),operation,release.map(sql_number).transpose()?,previous.map(sql_number).transpose()?,sql_number(generation)?,sql_number(revision)?,summary.to_string()])?;
    let id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
    for item in items {
        tx.conn().execute(
            "INSERT INTO _release_event_items VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",
            params![id, item.key, canonical_json(item)],
        )?;
    }
    Ok(revision)
}

impl Store {
    pub fn dormant_inventory(
        &mut self,
        after: Option<&str>,
        limit: usize,
                }
            }
            let population_changed=tx.meta("activation_population")?.as_deref()==Some("true");
            let rebuild: Vec<_> = schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m| {
                (changed && (population_changed || previous.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| crate::successor::maintain_rebuild_needed(old, schema, m))))
                    || (repair_live_sums && matches!(m.kind, crate::schema::MaintainKind::Sum { .. }) && schema.table(&m.source).is_some_and(|source| source.is_expiring()))
            }).collect();
            tx.set_meta("live_sum_candidates", "1")?;
            let attribution = recorded::paths_changed(schema, previous.as_ref());
            let columns = column_activation::changed_parents(schema, if population_changed {None} else {previous.as_ref()});
            if rebuild.is_empty() && !attribution && columns.is_empty() { return Ok(()); }
            let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
            let phase = std::time::Instant::now();
            if attribution {
                // This runner owns only activation rewrites; ordinary rebuilds
                // below retain their commit and partition accounting.
                let mut capture = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
                capture.capture_activation(previous.as_ref(), Some(report))?;
                report.detail("capture recorded paths", phase);
                capture.rebuild_recorded_activation(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild.iter().copied().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), report)?;
            }
            let mut runner = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
            runner.activate_columns(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild, &columns, Some(report))?;
            for maintain in rebuild.into_iter().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()) {
                if !recorded::table_paths_changed(schema.table(&maintain.source), previous.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.table(&maintain.source))) {
                    runner.rebuild_maintain_reported(maintain, report)?;
                }
            }
            let events = runner.events.take();
            let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
            let written = runner.rows_written;
            drop(runner);
            if written > 0 || !columns.is_empty() {
                tx.record_commit(seq, now, "rebuild", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "rebuild", &tables, written)?;
                partition::append(tx, seq, &events)?;
            }
            Ok(())
        }, |tx| {
            if let Some(options)=release {
                crate::store::release_history::finalize_restorations(tx)?;
                let mut items=crate::store::release_history::inventory(tx.conn(),None,usize::MAX)?;
                let dormant_items=items.len();
                let mut restored_items=0usize;
                for item in &mut items {
                    let before=prior_items.borrow().iter().find(|i|i.key==item.key).cloned();
                    let count=before.as_ref().map_or(0,|i|i.rows);
                    item.before_rows=Some(count);item.after_rows=Some(item.rows);
                    item.before_sample_ids=Some(before.map_or_else(Vec::new,|i|i.sample_ids));
                    let restored=if item.kind=="rows" {
                        tx.meta(&format!("activation_narrowed_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else if item.kind=="column" {tx.meta(&format!("activation_column_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)} else {0};
                    item.restored_rows=Some(restored);
                    if restored>0 {item.action="partially restored".into();restored_items+=1;}
                }
                let remaining:std::collections::BTreeSet<_>=items.iter().map(|i|i.key.clone()).collect();
                for mut item in prior_items.borrow().iter().filter(|i|!remaining.contains(&i.key)).cloned() {
                    let restored=if matches!(item.kind.as_str(),"rows"|"column") {
                        let prefix=if item.kind=="rows" {"activation_narrowed_restored"} else {"activation_column_restored"};
                        tx.meta(&format!("{prefix}:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else if item.kind=="table" {
                        tx.meta(&format!("activation_table_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else {item.rows};
                    item.action=if restored>0 || item.definition_only {"restored"} else {"removed without restoration"}.into();
                    item.before_rows=Some(item.rows);item.after_rows=Some(0);item.restored_rows=Some(restored);item.before_sample_ids=Some(item.sample_ids.clone());
                    if restored>0 || item.definition_only {restored_items+=1;}
                    items.push(item);
                }
                population.set(Some((tx.meta("activation_narrowed_retained")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),tx.meta("activation_narrowed_restored")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0))));
                crate::store::release_history::event(tx,&options.operation,Some(options.release),options.expected_previous,serde_json::json!({"dormant_items":dormant_items,"restored_items":restored_items,"native_digest":options.native_digest,"narrowed_retained":tx.meta("activation_narrowed_retained")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"narrowed_restored":tx.meta("activation_narrowed_restored")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)}),&items)?;
                tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key IN ('activation_restore','activation_population','activation_narrowed_retained','activation_narrowed_restored') OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%'",[])?;
            }
            crate::store::opening::Metadata::read(tx)
        });
        if outcome.is_ok() {
            if let Some((retained,restored))=population.get().filter(|(a,b)|*a>0 || *b>0) {
                store.adoption_report.changes.insert(0,format!("{retained} newly dormant {}; {restored} narrowed {} restored", if retained == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, if restored == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }));
            }
        }
        if outcome.is_ok() {
            if let Ok(items)=store.dormant_inventory(None,usize::MAX) {
                if release.is_some() { store.adoption_report.changes.insert(0, crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items)); }
            }
        }
        outcome
    }
}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/dormant_tests.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/dormant_tests.rs
index 2885f1f111..7fa6045eb6 100644

    /// One snapshot: current identity, bounded history and storage inventories.
    pub fn release_status(
        &mut self,
        after: u64,
        items_after: Option<&str>,
        event_id: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let mut events = Vec::new();
        let mut inventory_page = Vec::<serde_json::Value>::new();
        if present(tx.conn())? {
            let mut s=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT id,at,operation,release,previous,generation,revision,summary FROM _release_events WHERE id>?1 ORDER BY id LIMIT 16")?;
            let mut rows = s.query([sql_number(after)?])?;
            while let Some(r) = rows.next()? {
                events.push(serde_json::json!({"id":r.get::<_,i64>(0)?,"at":r.get::<_,i64>(1)?,"operation":r.get::<_,String>(2)?,"release":r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(3)?,"previous":r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(4)?,"generation":r.get::<_,i64>(5)?,"revision":r.get::<_,i64>(6)?,"summary":serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&r.get::<_,String>(7)?).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?}));
            }
            if let Some(event) = event_id {
                let mut s=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT detail FROM _release_event_items WHERE event=?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR path>?2) ORDER BY path LIMIT 16")?;
                for item in s.query_map(params![sql_number(event)?, items_after], |r| {
                    r.get::<_, String>(0)
                })? {
                    inventory_page.push(
                        serde_json::from_str(&item?)
                            .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?,
                    );
                }
            } else {
                inventory_page = inventory(tx.conn(), items_after, 16)?
                    .into_iter()
                    .map(|i| serde_json::to_value(i).unwrap())
                    .collect();
            }
        }
        let workers:Vec<(String,String)>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key='worker:status' OR key LIKE 'worker:status:%' ORDER BY key LIMIT 16")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        let previous_active: Option<u64> = if present(tx.conn())? {tx.conn().query_row("SELECT previous FROM _release_events WHERE release IS NOT NULL AND previous != release AND operation != 'prune' ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1",[],|r|r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(0)).optional()?.flatten().map(|n|n as u64)} else {None};
        let result = serde_json::json!({"previous_active":previous_active,"release":tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()),"revision":tx.meta("release_revision")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"generation":tx.meta("generation")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(1),"native_digest":tx.meta("release_native_digest")?,"events_after":events.last().map(|e|e["id"].clone()),"events":events,"items_after":inventory_page.last().map(|i|i["key"].clone()),"items":inventory_page,"event":event_id,"workers":workers});
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(result)
    }
    pub fn prune_dormant(
        &mut self,
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.transaction()?;
        let held = tx
            .meta("release_revision")?
            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
            .unwrap_or(0);
        if expected_revision.is_some_and(|n| n != held) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation(
                "release revision changed; run releases and retry prune".into(),
            ));
        }
        let items = inventory(tx.conn(), None, usize::MAX)?;
        if !confirm {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(
                serde_json::json!({"pruned":false,"items":items,"rewrite":"snapback4 prune <dir> --allow-destructive"}),
            );
        }
        if !present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":[],"revision":held}));
        }
        // Release only dormant homes; active application data is untouched.
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE home_table IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=v.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column AND v.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
        if super::narrowed::present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_values.t AND n.id=_dormant_values.id)",[])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_narrow_rows",[])?;
        }
        let revision = event(
            &tx,
            "prune",
            tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v| v.parse().ok()),
            None,
            serde_json::json!({"dropped_items":items.len(),"bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.bytes).sum::<u64>(),"asset_bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.asset_bytes).sum::<u64>()}),
            &items,
        )?;
        let event_id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
        tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _dormant_values;DELETE FROM _dormant_rows;DELETE FROM _dormant_refs;DELETE FROM _dormant_columns;DELETE FROM _dormant_indexes;DELETE FROM _dormant_tables;")?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":items,"revision":revision,"event":event_id}))
    }
}
